## Bounded material-fact pass

9. **One finite pass, pre-declared and then closed.** The bounded-research rule permits a single material-fact pass for genuinely disputed points or for a short pre-declared list of standard connecting names and dates whose omission would create a real syllabus gap. The list was fixed at four items before the pass began, and the pass was closed after them.
   - **Item one, the Chandigarh authorship chain.** The canonical record contains one sentence - that the Punjab government hired Le Corbusier, a French architect, to design Chandigarh - which invites a false inference of sole authorship. A single reference check established that an initial master plan was prepared by Albert Mayer working with Matthew Nowicki, that Nowicki's death in 1950 ended that phase, and that Le Corbusier then led the revision and implementation with Pierre Jeanneret, Maxwell Fry and Jane Drew, replacing the earlier layout with a rectilinear grid of sectors and placing the Capitol Complex at the head of the plan. This is carried as a reference-level finding, is labelled as such in Lesson 10, and is not extended into sector dimensions, road hierarchies, population targets or any planning analysis.
   - **Item two, Louis Kahn's Indian commission.** The canonical record already names Louis Kahn, with Le Corbusier, as an influence on this topic's Pritzker laureate, but describes no Indian work of his, which would leave the influence unexplained. A single reference check established that Kahn was commissioned in 1962 for the Indian Institute of Management at Ahmedabad on the recommendation of B. V. Doshi, who acted as Indian associate architect; that Vikram Sarabhai and the founding group were central to the institute's establishment; that construction continued into the 1970s and that Kahn died in 1974; and that the campus is widely known for load-bearing brick and large geometric openings. Carried as reference-level, labelled in Lesson 12, with no award, dimension, cost or completion year claimed.
   - **Item three, the public-works generation.** The canonical record's post-1947 set moves directly from a 1958 landmark commission to individually celebrated architects, which conceals the institutional route by which state architecture changed hands. A single reference check established that Habib Rahman **joined the Central Public Works Department in 1953 as Senior Architect and later rose to Chief Architect**, trained at the Massachusetts Institute of Technology in the Bauhaus tradition, and is associated with Rabindra Bhavan in New Delhi, the New Secretariat Building in Kolkata recorded as India's first steel-framed high-rise, and low-cost housing for refugees after Partition. No year for the promotion to Chief Architect was established, so none is written anywhere in this session. National honours are commonly attributed to him; the official Government of India Padma Awards record returned an access error and could not be consulted, so **no award is claimed for him anywhere in this session**, in any year or class, and Lesson 11 states that restriction in ordinary teaching terms.
   - **Item four, the four status facts this topic's own owners flagged for verification.** The canonical owners record the Mumbai Victorian Gothic and Art Deco ensembles as a World Heritage property while explicitly instructing that boundary and criteria specifics be verified against the property's own official page before citing, and they name no railway building at all. The official World Heritage records for three properties were therefore opened directly: the Victorian Gothic and Art Deco Ensembles of Mumbai, inscribed 2018, supplying the two-wave urban history, the 1880s Victorian Gothic group and the creation of the Oval Maidan, the early-twentieth-century Backbay Reclamation Scheme with its Art Deco fabric and the Marine Drive sea front, the east-west orientation across the Oval Maidan, the Indo-Gothic blending with balconies and verandas introduced in response to the local climate, the Indo-Deco formulation and its subcontinental spread, the Rajabai Clock Tower as the visual high point, and the Heritage Regulations for Greater Bombay 1995 with the Fort and Marine Drive precincts; the Chhatrapati Shivaji Terminus, inscribed 2004, supplying F. W. Stevens as architect, the 2.85 hectare extent, the ten-year build from 1878, the first-terminus-in-the-subcontinent status, the stone dome, turrets, pointed arches and eccentric ground plan close to traditional Indian palace architecture, the record of British architects working with Indian craftsmen to forge a style unique to Mumbai, and the advanced structural and technical solutions; and The Architectural Work of Le Corbusier, inscribed 2016 as a serial transnational property of seventeen sites in seven countries on three continents, supplying the Capitol Complex's recorded influence across the subcontinent and its symbolism of India's accession to modernity, its sunscreens, double-skinned roofs and reflecting pools adopted for natural air-conditioning and energy saving, the use of the Modulor for its exterior spaces, its status as the focal point of the city plan and as the most complete contribution to the Radiant City idea, and the record that the Unité d'Habitation at Marseille pioneered the trend of Brutalism.
10. No other external research was conducted for factual material. No optional source, exhaustive corroboration or decorative current affairs was pursued, and the pass was closed after these four items.

## Current-affairs source used

11. **Lesson 7, the single current linkage.** Search run: "Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj Terminus heritage restoration 2026 and Mumbai colonial-era heritage conservation". Item used: *Free Press Journal*, "Mumbai: Porous Stones, Ageing Masonry Joints Behind CSMT Dome Leak; Experts Roped In For Heritage Repairs", **published 12 July 2026**, read directly. Recorded content used: that leakage from the dome of the Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj Terminus during ongoing restoration was traced by the Rail Land Development Authority to porous stones and ageing masonry joints; that stone joints had been treated using lime-and-jaggery-based grout and gravity grouting, a traditional conservation technique recommended by the Mumbai Heritage Conservation Committee; that sealing porous stones requires specialised treatment complying with heritage conservation norms, for which technical inputs were sought from structural consultants at the Veermata Jijabai Technological Institute and from conservationists affiliated with that committee; and the authority's quoted statement that the work "is in progress and will be completed by August 31, 2026". The item is attributed to that dated newspaper report of an authority's statement; no primary conservation document was opened, and the item asserts nothing about the building's present condition or about conservation policy.
12. No other current item is used anywhere in this session. Seventeen of the eighteen lessons record truthfully in their pre-teach checklists that no current linkage is required.

## Evidence, discrepancy and prohibition controls

- **Disclosed source discrepancy one - the upturned dome and the Supreme Court.** The core source records the upturned dome as introduced during the post-1911 Neo-Roman phase and cites it atop the Supreme Court and Rashtrapati Bhavan; this topic's canonical Basic owner separately records the Supreme Court of India, 1958, as G. B. Deolalikar's commission and as the first major post-Independence state commission by an Indian architect. The conflict is stated openly in Lessons 8 and 11 and recorded in the error map and register notes. It is not resolved by dropping either statement: the device is treated as Raj-period, the building as a 1958 Indian commission, and the pair is taught as the topic's sharpest instance of a formal device outliving the political order that introduced it.
- **Disclosed source discrepancy two - the location of the upturned dome.** The core source names the Supreme Court and Rashtrapati Bhavan; this topic's canonical Basic owner writes the pair as the Supreme Court and the old Parliament or Rashtrapati Bhavan complex. This session follows the core source's exact naming and records the owner's wider formulation rather than silently merging the two.
- **Disclosed source discrepancy three - the Chandigarh authorship sentence.** The canonical record's single sentence implies sole authorship; the bounded material-fact pass established a prior plan and an implementing team. The session states both, uses the canonical sentence as the recorded fact and the pass finding as reference-level qualification, and instructs that sole authorship not be asserted.
- **Disclosed coverage boundary - cantonment, hill-station and public-health segregation.** These standard colonial-urbanism themes are not carried as described architectural material in this topic's owners or in the core art source. The session reports the boundary explicitly in Lesson 7, supplies the recorded phrase "monumental administrative vistas and segregated civic cores" as the only owned segregation evidence, routes the sanitary and municipal history to Modern History, and forbids the supply of named examples from memory. This is recorded as a gap in the evidence base, not as a gap in coverage.
- **Disclosed coverage boundary - the Dutch and Danish.** Both powers are named in the core source's five-power contest and neither has any described building, style, town or technique. Lesson 2 teaches the asymmetry as itself examinable and supplies the safe formulation; no Dutch or Danish example is supplied anywhere.
- **Disclosed evidence limit - the two post-1947 schools.** The core source names the Revivalist and Modernist schools without defining either, without dating the boundary between them and without assigning any architect to either. Lesson 9 states that limit, offers only the minimal reading the labels themselves license and marks it as a reading, and prohibits the allocation of named architects to either school.
- **Award prohibitions observed absolutely.** No award of any kind is attributed to Raj Rewal, and the previously recorded false "Padma Shri 1989" is named as a correction rather than repeated; the commonly attributed institute gold medal and Robert Mathew Award are recorded as not carried in this evidence base and are not cited as fact. No award is claimed for Habib Rahman, because the official Padma record could not be consulted. No Padma award is attributed to Laurie Baker, and his Pritzker status is always written as a 2006 nomination that did not result in an award. The string "Padma Vibhushan 2020" appears nowhere in this session except as an explicitly named prohibition; the 2020 award is written as the Padma Bhushan and the 2023 posthumous Padma Vibhushan is retained.
- **Attribution prohibitions observed absolutely.** New Delhi and the 1927 Parliament building are attributed to Lutyens and Baker jointly throughout, and no individual New Delhi building is assigned to either separately. The Supreme Court is never attributed to Lutyens, Baker or Le Corbusier. No architect or date is supplied for the Madras High Court or the Mysore Palace. Chandigarh is never described as the work of an Indian architect. No further Kanvinde building is named beyond the four recorded and the source's own "many other buildings" clause. No work, date or award is added for B. V. Doshi beyond the official citation and the official Padma record.
- **Hedges preserved.** The Mitaoli influence is written as a debated resemblance throughout and is never attached to the 2023 building; the official 2021 Preliminary item's own phrase "popular belief" is reproduced as the licensing register. Aranya's figures are written as "over 6,500 residences" and "over 80,000 individuals", retaining the citation's own "over". Kanvinde's Brutalism is always "elements of". The Hall of Nations and the Asian Games Village are written as works "associated with" their architect. Le Corbusier's nationality is reproduced as the record's own description rather than asserted independently.
- **Learner-facing register.** Generation, verification and research-pass language is confined to this ledger. In the lessons, the same limits are expressed as ordinary evidence statements - what is carried, what is not carried, and what may not be supplied.
- **Numerical prohibitions observed absolutely.** No cost, floor area or dimension is stated for any colonial structure beyond the recorded 2.85 hectare extent of the terminus. No structural system, span or dimension is stated for the Hall of Nations, and its demolition is dated only to the year. No quantified saving is claimed for filler slab construction or rat-trap bond. No World Heritage totals, protected-monument counts, budgets or danger-listings appear anywhere.
- **Interpretive openness preserved.** The appropriation reading of Indo-Gothic is presented as a qualified analytical judgement with the climatic counter-consideration attached. The three readings of the Chandigarh paradox are offered as readings. The critical-regionalism label is defined as external interpretive vocabulary and is never written as a recorded classification. Every symbolic reading of plan, dome, axis or orientation is marked as a reading.
- **Status-claim discipline.** Inscription years and recorded inscription content are asserted only where they identify a building's architectural character - 2004 for the terminus, 2018 for the Mumbai ensembles, 2016 for the Le Corbusier serial property. The World Heritage status of the Basilica of Bom Jesus is named as recorded without its inscription year or property name. All counts, protection statuses, conservation law, institutional structure and management arrangements are routed to Topic 14.
- **Boundary discipline.** The medieval sense of "Indo-Saracenic" is named once and routed to Topic 04. Chandigarh as a planned-city case study is routed to Geography and Governance. The 1911 capital transfer as a political decision, the colonial political and economic timeline, and the Central Vista project as policy, cost and controversy are routed to Modern History and Governance. Heritage law, the antiquity threshold's statutory operation, institutions, counts and reform proposals are routed to Topic 14. Freestanding sculpture and iconographic doctrine are routed to Topic 06.
- **Bounded-research discipline.** The substantive base is the complete canonical Basic owner, the complete canonical Advanced owner and one core OCR art source. External work was limited to exact PYQ verification and routing, one pre-declared four-item material-fact pass, and one brief dated current linkage, after which the pass was closed.
